# Proposed OEIS comments: A224004 to A229508

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A224004

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A224004>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [3892](../papers/03501-04000/03892-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = (1/3628800)*n^12 + (1/302400)*n^11 + (419/3628800)*n^10 + (223/120960)*n^9 + (35411/1209600)*n^8 + (20443/50400)*n^7 + (17616857/3628800)*n^6 + (6342323/120960)*n^5 + (821276033/1814400)*n^4 + (103253527/37800)*n^3 + (186412339/16800)*n^2 - (24901729/840)*n - 64275 for n>8

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical closed form is correct. The count is a walk count in a finite graph, so it satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence; the conjectured form satisfies the same one, and the two agree at enough consecutive indices to agree from then on, for n > 21. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A224007

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A224007>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [5065](../papers/05001-05500/05065-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 10*a(n-1) - 16*a(n-2) + 30*a(n-3) - 14*a(n-4) + 12*a(n-5).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(5 + 14*x^2 - x^3 + 6*x^4) / (1 - 10*x + 16*x^2 - 30*x^3 + 14*x^4 - 12*x^5). - _Colin Barker_, Aug 26 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 80 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-1) - 2*a(n-2) - a(n-3) - 4*a(n-4) + 14*a(n-5) - 2*a(n-6) - 2*a(n-9).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(7 - 9*x^2 - 12*x^3 + 10*x^4 - 2*x^8) / (1 - 4*x + 2*x^2 + x^3 + 4*x^4 - 14*x^5 + 2*x^6 + 2*x^9). - _Colin Barker_, Aug 28 2018

```

```

## A224350

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A224350>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [13327](../papers/13001-13500/13327-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 56 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 242 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 56, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A224387

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A224387>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [13368](../papers/13001-13500/13368-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 47 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 245 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 47, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A224744

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A224744>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [6701](../papers/06501-07000/06701-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 55 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 312 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 55, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A225007

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A225007>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4914](../papers/04501-05000/04914-PROOF.pdf)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 16 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

## A225311

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A225311>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [1738](../papers/01501-02000/01738-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = a(n-1) + a(n-2) - 2*a(n-5) + a(n-8) + a(n-9) - a(n-10).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(2 + 5*x + 7*x^2 + 8*x^3 + 5*x^4 + 4*x^5 + x^6 + 2*x^7 + x^8 - x^9) / ((1 - x)^4*(1 + x)^2*(1 + x^2)*(1 + x + x^2)). - _Colin Barker_, Sep 05 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true, and the arrays can be dispensed with entirely. A nondecreasing row of -1s and 1s is a block of -1s followed by a block of 1s, so it is fixed by one number k_i, the count of -1s; the row sums to n - 2k_i and the condition Sum_{i,j} i*x(i,j) = 0 becomes Sum_i i*k_i = n*H*(H+1)/4. So a(n) counts the lattice points of the box {0..n}^H on one hyperplane -- the points of the n-th dilate of a fixed rational polytope -- and is therefore a quasi-polynomial in n. A quasi-polynomial of degree d and period P satisfies the recurrence with characteristic polynomial (x^P - 1)^(d+1); here d <= H-1 and P divides 2*lcm(1..H), so the order is at most 96. Checking the recurrence above against that bound is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 9. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [13407](../papers/13001-13500/13407-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: +(95*n+537)*(n+2)*a(n) +(95*n^2-16421*n-14748) *a(n-1) +(-6403*n^2+124495*n-60066) *a(n-2) +(21565*n^2-354883*n+596496) *a(n-3) +2*(-5092*n^2+138877*n-395970) *a(n-4) +8*(-2470*n^2+11113*n+12744) *a(n-5) +192*(38*n-67)*(2*n-13)*a(n-6)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 14 2016

*No comment drafted for this entry.*

## A226751

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A226751>  
Result obtained: **30 August 2026**  
Paper: [12740](../papers/12501-13000/12740-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: 18*n*(2*n-1)*(55*n-76)*a(n) +(-11605*n^3+28521*n^2-20870*n+4536)*a(n-1) -24*(55*n-21)*(3*n-4)*(3*n-2)*a(n-2)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 14 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The entry posts no generating function, but its definition a(n) = [x^n] f(x)g(x)^n gives one: reading the extraction as a contour integral and summing the geometric series leaves a single integral whose only pole inside a small circle is the branch of x = t*g(x) through the origin, and the residue there is an algebraic function. The recurrence then follows from it, for n > 1. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A226866

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A226866>  
Result obtained: **13 September 2026**  
Paper: [4935](../papers/04501-05000/04935-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = (1/40)*n^5 + (7/8)*n^3 + (21/10)*n + 1.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Sep 06 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence follows from the generating function this entry already states as fact: a recurrence with constant coefficients is exactly a denominator of the generating function, so the two are the same statement, and it holds for n > 6. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A228278

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A228278>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [5204](../papers/05001-05500/05204-PROOF.pdf)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 8 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A228329

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A228329>  
Result obtained: **30 August 2026**  
Paper: [12858](../papers/12501-13000/12858-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: n*(2*n+1)*a(n) + 2*(-26*n^2+25*n-11)*a(n-1) + 20*(4*n-5)*(4*n-7)*a(n-2) = 0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Sep 08 2013

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the closed form already on the entry. It is a sum of finitely many hypergeometric terms, so every shift quotient is a rational function of n and the recurrence collapses to one rational identity in n, which is true. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A228330

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A228330>  
Result obtained: **26 August 2026**  
Paper: [13791](../papers/13501-14000/13791-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: n*(2*n+1)*(3467*n-4029)*a(n) + 8*(-36721*n^3 + 109040*n^2 - 137926*n + 69822)*a(n-1) + 4*(4*n-9)*(45706*n-7907)*(4*n-7)*a(n-2) = 0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Sep 08 2013

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the recurrence already stated on the entry, which is not labelled a conjecture: in the Ore algebra Q(n)[N], N the shift, the conjectured operator is a left multiple of that one, so it annihilates the sequence as well. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Empirical g.f.: x*(1 - x)*(3 + 2*x) / (1 - 2*x - 7*x^2 + 2*x^3 + 3*x^4). - _Colin Barker_, Sep 12 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 16 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A228960

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A228960>  
Result obtained: **26 August 2026**  
Paper: [13786](../papers/13501-14000/13786-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: 3*n*(1661*n-1820) *(3*n-1) *(3*n-2) *a(n) +(1927258*n^4 -17091925*n^3 +50171975*n^2 -59448794*n +24442440) *a(n-1) +12*(-705605*n^4 +6363374*n^3 -20228575*n^2 +27219817*n -13185456) *a(n-2) +54*(n-2) *(250934*n^3 -1892927*n^2 +4367836*n -3055963) *a(n-3) -486*(n-2)*(n-3) *(26090*n-34343) *(2*n-7) *a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Sep 15 2013.
> Conjecture 1: the supercongruence a(n*p^r) == a(n*p^(r-1)) (mod p^(2*r)) holds for positive integers n and r and all primes p >= 5. Cf. A350383.
> Conjecture 2: let k be a positive integer, m an integer and let f(x) = g(x)/h(x), where both g(x) and h(x) are finite products of cyclotomic polynomials. Then the same supercongruences hold, except for a finite number of primes p depending on f(x), for the sequence {a_(k,m,f)(n): n >= 0} defined by a_(k,m,f)(n) = [x^(k*n)] f(x)^(m*n). (End)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the recurrence already stated on the entry, which is not labelled a conjecture: in the Ore algebra Q(n)[N], N the shift, the conjectured operator is a left multiple of that one, so it annihilates the sequence as well. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A229397

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A229397>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [12262](../papers/12001-12500/12262-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 5*a(n-1) - 6*a(n-2) + 3*a(n-3) - a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(4 - 7*x + 3*x^2 - x^3) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 4*x + 2*x^2 - x^3)). - _Colin Barker_, Sep 14 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition is carried by a bounded amount of state down the array -- one bit per column where it asks for a parity reaching back to the top, and otherwise a window of three consecutive lines, since every cell it names lies one step away -- so counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 5 states, and a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

